When we look at the last 20 years, Turkish agriculture and Turkish farmer's share of the economy are getting smaller and smaller. For example, in 1999, Turkey, which is the fifth in the world in terms of agricultural additive value, fell to the 10th place today, and Turkey, which ranked 17th in land efficiency, fell to the 23rd place. We see all these decreases happening both in dry agriculture and in juicy agriculture, both in fruit and in grain. We also see that the agricultural sector is involved in lower income groups in the division of national income per unit. In Turkey, national income per milliliter in the agricultural sector is one third of the average national income. So we're talking about $8,500 in average national revenues, about $2,500 per fert, 3,000 dollars in revenues in agriculture. This does not fall below the average national income in other countries, but less than half of it. For example, for a month's student cost, an agricultural sector employee should sell 92 litres of milk in 2009, while in 2017 the amount of milk his child should sell for a month's education is 155 litres. There's a setback. One of the major centers of the agricultural sector in Turkey, as we all know, is Burma. There are about 344,000 hectares of agricultural land in Burma, and there are 22,000 hectares of fruit and 41,000 hectares of plant vegetables. The agricultural sector is very victimised by costs in Bursa, and all over Turkey. Mr. President, valued lawmakers, there is a structural link between the rule of law and judicial independence and neutrality. We see the guarantee of judicial independence and neutrality in democracies as a prerequisite for the rule of law. This is also a guarantee of the basic rights and freedoms of individuals. There are many basic principles for the establishment of judicial independence, but close to all of these basic principles, it basically serves to strengthen judges and prosecutors. With the Constitutional amendment of our nation's approval as EC PARTY in 2017, we based the structure of the Council of Judges and Prosecutors on the principles of independence and neutrality.
